## Why this file exists

It was **item 85 of itself**. The backlog was produced by a review that called
it "the most valuable artifact of that session", and it then lived in an
untracked text file in the operator's home directory that no gate read. CLAUDE.md
section 1 states the consequence in general terms:

> "A DEFERRAL THAT LIVES ONLY IN PROSE HAS NOT BEEN DEFERRED -- IT HAS BEEN
> DROPPED. The ITEM LEDGER works for exactly one reason: a test reads it."

The proof arrived on 2026-08-12: a session with full authority to work the
backlog could not find it, and the operator had to say where it was. That is the
whole failure mode -- not that the list was wrong, but that nothing could see it.

`tests/test_v3_66_1052_the_backlog_is_machine_visible.py` now reads this file.

## THIS IS THE ONLY LIVE TASK NUMBERING

These IDs are the backlog's own canonical namespace. Older session-ledger,
tracker, plan, and spec numbering is historical evidence in Git, never a second
place to file or close work. When citing a task, say "backlog N".

## Format, which the gate depends on

One row per item: `| <id> | <status>[ @<evidence>] | <text> |`

`OPEN` carries no evidence. `CLOSED` and `MOOT` must carry it -- a version
(`@1049`) or a commit -- because a close nobody can check is a claim, not a
record. Status is one of `OPEN`, `CLOSED`, `MOOT`.

`MOOT` is deliberately distinct from `CLOSED`: it means the SUBJECT went away
rather than the work being done. Collapsing the two would let a disappeared
problem read as a solved one.

A `CLOSED` row MAY carry an unfinished remainder, but it must say so in the
status: `CLOSED @NNNN -- PARTIAL, and the remainder is named`. Rows 3, 89, 90,
95 and 97 are the worked examples. The status column is the only thing a machine
reads, so a remainder living in a row's TEXT under a bare `CLOSED` is invisible
to every count and every triage -- see row 143.

| 28 | CLOSED @1194 | a WRITE-recorder: the filesystem analogue of the socket recorder  REOPENED @1113 AS A FALSE CLOSURE, and it is the inverse of row 91. This row was marked `CLOSED @1055`; `git show` of that cut has the row flipping OPEN to CLOSED with its TEXT UNCHANGED, and what @1055 actually shipped was `bd-modwatch` -- a `sys.modules` TABLE watcher. A module-table watcher and a filesystem-write recorder share nothing but the word "watch". VERIFIED at f0c5667: no filesystem write recorder exists among the 254 tools in `toolchain/bin`. THE PAIR IS THE POINT: row 91 sat OPEN through the two cuts that fixed it, and this row read CLOSED for a cut that did not touch it, so the register has now been measured wrong in BOTH directions -- which is why row 105 (neither tracker gate runs in CI) is the structural fix rather than any individual correction. If the subject is genuinely unwanted, MOOT it with that reasoning written down; do not re-close it on @1055 CLOSED HONESTLY @1194: `toolchain/bin/bd-writerec` is the filesystem analogue the row asked for, shipped as an opt-in Linux/strace evidence lane rather than runtime authority. It follows fork/clone/vfork/clone3 descendants, preserves per-PID raw logs, normalizes successful and failed mutating syscalls to deterministic JSONL, resolves fd-relative paths where the trace proves them, and preserves workload/tracer exit witnesses. Recorder-owned raw bytes and termination are bounded; every refusal publishes atomic schema-shaped UNKNOWN, and limit/exception cleanup proves the tracer process group empty. The contract explicitly declares truncated tails, unresolved descriptors, MAP_SHARED/mmap, io_uring, and namespace/container visibility as blind spots. Synthetic trace fixtures and live process-tree controls are guarded by a 24/24 non-equivalent executable mutation battery. It is deliberately not wired into `capture.sh`; evidence-only means absence or UNKNOWN cannot authorize a product verdict |

| 100 | CLOSED @1092 | THE @1079 TREE GUARD CHECKED PREFLIGHT ONLY, so it cannot see the failure it was written for. `capture.sh` refuses a tree that is DIRTY WHEN THE RUN STARTS (scripts/lib/tree_state.sh, backlog 98) and that is the whole check -- a tree that goes dirty DURING the run passes it. The guard shipped HOURS BEFORE that happened and could not have caught it. THE SHAPE GENERALISES: a gate whose denominator is one INSTANT cannot answer a question about an INTERVAL -- section 0's denominator rule in the time dimension rather than the set dimension. Candidate fix: record the tree sha at preflight and re-check it immediately before the graph gate, naming the paths that moved; tree_state.sh already computes both halves, so this is a second call site rather than a new predicate. The postflight half now exists: `bd_tree_state_snapshot` records HEAD plus the porcelain listing after the preflight check passes, `bd_tree_state_drift` compares at the end, and a non-empty drift file grades the run INVALID (exit 3) rather than FAIL -- a THIRD verdict state, because spending the word FAIL on a void measurement is exactly how the 1082 round was read as a code defect over a green suite. HEAD is in the snapshot because a mid-run COMMIT leaves `git status` empty on both sides while moving the source the graph pin is derived from. UNKNOWN stays a third state at both ends: an unreadable tree returns nonzero rather than an empty snapshot, since two empty snapshots compare EQUAL and would certify no-drift over a subject nothing could see. CAPTURE_ALLOW_DIRTY does NOT suppress it -- the override consents to a known state at t=0, not to a tree that moves mid-run. MEASURED before shipping: all four hosts read 0 dirty paths after the 2026-08-13 capture round and tools/deployed_version.txt is gitignored, so the check does not fire on a normal run; 6/6 mutants caught, including one that drops the trailing-newline normalisation, which the over-sensitivity control caught live during this cut when it would have made EVERY capture report INVALID |

| 104 | CLOSED @1191 | Test roots are classified by their atomic marker and kernel-released lock, never by stale directory mtime alone. `bd-gc` refuses LIVE and UNKNOWN roots; keeps the newest 20 at-risk KEPT_FOR_FORENSICS or ABANDONED roots independently of every root younger than 24 hours; and admits a RECLAIMABLE clean root only after that floor. `/tmp/bd-runctx` is structurally excluded. The same cut scopes automatic capture/wedge entry sweeps away from mtime-only families and includes both lock-classified `bd-testrun-*` objects and secrets-bearing keyed capture vaults while live `bdcut_*` and `pytest-of-*` remain explicit operator actions. | 145 | CLOSED @1126 | AN XDIST WORKER THAT HARD-EXITS DURING THE DRAIN LIVELOCKS THE MASTER, AND THE MECHANISM IS NOW READ, REPRODUCED AND CONFIRMED. Was the second half of row 144. `LoadScopeScheduling.tests_finished` counts a work unit with FEWER THAN TWO pending tests as finished (`if self._pending_of(assigned_unit) >= 2: return False`), so once every node is down to its LAST test the session declares itself done and `triggershutdown()` latches every worker via `WorkerController.shutdown()` -- WHILE THOSE TESTS ARE STILL RUNNING. The latch is `shutting_down = self._down or self._shutdown_sent`: set once, never cleared. A worker then dies on that last test (pytest-timeout's thread method calls `os._exit(1)`), `worker_errordown` re-queues its work and clones a replacement, but `_reschedule` refuses every latched node (`if node.shutting_down: return`), so the re-queued units can never be dispatched. `has_pending` stays True, `tests_finished` stays False, and `triggershutdown()` is reachable only AFTER `queue.get()` returns an event -- while the clone keeps `_active_nodes` non-empty, disarming the only other exit. Both exits closed, no further event possible, master spins on `queue.get(timeout=2.0)` forever AWAKE beside idle workers. EVIDENCE AT THREE LEVELS: (1) read live with gdb out of a master wedged 11.6 hours -- `_active_nodes=['gw48']`, workqueue 8 units UNDISPATCHED, tests_finished False, has_pending True; (2) a minimal reproducer at `upstream/xdist-drain-livelock/` -- hangs 5/5, control (same tree, `os._exit` replaced by `pass`) passes 5/5 in ~4s, does NOT reproduce at -n 4, `--max-worker-restart=0` or `--dist load`; (3) production confirmation in `logs/test2-full-005-160601.log`, the first wedge captured after @1126's observability fixes, carrying `replacing crashed worker gw4` at line 31828 -- inside the ~4KB tail that buffering had eaten on all 15 earlier wedges. NOT FILED UPSTREAM by operator decision; the bundle is filing-ready in-repo. Six earlier hypotheses died by measurement before this one, and it is the first that was MEASURED BEFORE IT WAS BELIEVED |

Read the code: the WEDGE-CONFIRMED branch sends SIGINT, waits `sigint_grace`, re-runs forensics, and THEN sends `kill -9` to the process GROUP and the pid. It was correct throughout, and a fix aimed there would have changed nothing. THE ORPHANS CAME FROM THE BRANCHES THAT END A RUN WITHOUT KILLING IT, all four found by reading every terminal exit of the monitor loop at v3.66.1131: (1) INTERRUPT -- `main` caught KeyboardInterrupt, set STOP and RETURNED, and the host threads are `daemon=True`, so the interpreter killed them at exit; the remote run was never killed AND its row was never written. That is the whole of the five observed orphans, and it is why `rows.jsonl` holds ZERO abandoned rows: they were never mis-recorded, they were never recorded. (2) --hours said "letting in-flight samples finish" and did the opposite -- the loop is `while not STOP.is_set()`, so a sample drops out on the next tick and fell through to `setdefault("state", "COMPLETED")`, recording an abandoned run as a completed NON-WEDGE. A false negative in the denominator the whole hunt turns on. It never fired during the 2026-08-14 run -- MEASURED: 686 of 686 COMPLETED rows carry a real `pytest_exit` -- so no preserved row is contaminated and the 702 need no correction on this account. (3) CAPPED killed the master pid ONLY while the wedge branch four lines above killed the GROUP: same job, two spellings, up to 48 workers left behind. (4) UNKNOWN recorded "the run was NOT killed and may still be there" and did not try. THE FIX: one `reap_cmd(pid)` builder that resolves the process group and kills it, one `reap()` that runs it and RECORDS the outcome on the row (REAP-OK / REAP-SURVIVED / REAP-UNREACHABLE / REAP-NOPID), wired into all four paths; a new ABANDONED state guarded on the absence of `pytest_exit`; and a bounded `join` in the interrupt handler so each thread reaps and writes before exit. A DEFECT THE STRUCTURAL TESTS COULD NOT SEE, found by driving the real thing: the first `reap_cmd` used `kill -0` as its liveness probe and reported REAP-SURVIVED after correctly killing a five-process tree, because `kill -0` succeeds on a ZOMBIE -- a gate firing on correct work, which section 0 counts as a soundness bug. It now reads the process STATE and treats `Z` as gone, with an over-sensitivity twin asserting it can still report a genuine survivor. bd-mutate 5 caught / 0 escaped, and the first run ESCAPED one: `"join" in ast.unparse(handler)` was satisfied by the handler's own `", ".join(alive)`. MEASURED 2026-08-14 at the end of a ~19h hunt: FIVE orphaned masters across the fleet, the oldest 41614s (11.6 HOURS), with test4 carrying FIVE concurrent suites and test7 four before they were reaped. Each orphan is a master plus up to 48 idle workers. WHY THIS IS NOT MERELY UNTIDY: load is this bug's dominant covariate -- reduced-size arms are 0/73 against 15 of ~620 on full -- so unreaped masters progressively corrupt the very measurement the hunt exists to take, and they do it in the direction that INCREASES the apparent rate over time. Cross-host and cross-time contrasts drawn over a long hunt are suspect for exactly this reason and no correction has been applied to the 702 preserved rows. The hunt's own log says it plainly at shutdown: `interrupted -- setting STOP; in-flight samples are NOT killed`. THE FIX BELONGS WITH THE KILLER, the same conclusion row 104 reaches: whatever declares a run wedged is the only party that knows it died, so it should reap the process group after forensics, not hope a signal handler runs. A retention sweep at hunt START would also bound it, the way capture.sh prunes on the way IN (@1099) |

The two halves then degrade in OPPOSITE directions: the commit half has `|| echo unknown` and correctly reports `unknown`, while the dirty half has no fallback -- git writes nothing to stdout, its error is swallowed by `2>/dev/null`, `wc -l` counts zero lines, and the record reads `dirty=0`, which is the SAME STRING a genuinely clean tree produces. | 179 | CLOSED @1199 -- root cause was prune, not a worker-exit race | RUNCTX-RECORDER-PARTIAL-CAPTURE. The recorder captured a partial chain set under canonical `-n 24`: three v3.66.1199 diagnosis runs recorded 126, 151 and 171 files of ~1386 (20-22 of 24 chains), and the four earlier v3.66.1195 fleet observations ranged 111 to 173 files and 19 to 22 chains. ROOT CAUSE DIAGNOSED at v3.66.1199 and it is NOT the `flush/race at worker exit` this row hypothesised: `_run_context.prune` ranked run directories by DIRECTORY mtime and kept the newest 20, but a chain is append-only, so appending updates the FILE mtime and leaves the containing directory's frozen at the moment its 24 chains were created. A live suite's run dir then ranked stale, and any of the many tests that spawn a nested pytest triggered the shared `prune` on unconfigure, EVICTING the live outer run mid-suite -- all 24 chains vanished at once and only the post-eviction tail survived. Proven by instrumentation: 84 chain-wipe events across all workers within a 3-second window, preserved at `cut-179/wipe-trace-84-events.log`. FIX: rank by newest CONTENT mtime, so an actively-appended run always ranks newest and is never evicted; a full `-n 24` suite now records 24/24 chains and all 1388 files (`cut-179/full-suite-fixed.log`). RED-first eviction repro + 4/4 mutation battery + a negative control that keeps retention bounded at keep=20 (`tests/test_v3_66_1199_prune_never_evicts_a_live_run.py`). The consequences this row named are resolved: `bd-ladder` chain-absence and recorder-derived denominators are trustworthy again. Row 103's `48 chains for 48 workers` invariant should still be revisited -- it held only because those runs had no nested-pytest eviction. |

Missing, malformed, or mismatched claims remain UNKNOWN. Malformed, late-truncated, embedded, duplicate, conflicting, verdict/field-incoherent, or status-mismatched terminal evidence remains UNKNOWN; output with no terminal evidence remains unreachable, and neither can become `REAP-OK`. THE ADMIN API TOKEN CANNOT DO WHAT ITS NAME IMPLIES. An admin token enqueues and reads, but refuses real state-changing work -- notably it cannot generate tokens. WHY IT MATTERS BEYOND CONVENIENCE. A token that reads and enqueues but cannot administer is not an admin token, and the name is the whole problem: an operator reasonably expects it to work and discovers otherwise only at the moment they need it. Every additional host then requires manual token provisioning, which is exactly the step that does not scale to a fleet -- and the failure appears as a refusal at the point of use rather than at the point the token was issued. Acceptance: establish what an admin token is ACTUALLY permitted to do by reading the authorisation checks rather than the docs, then either (a) grant admin tokens the administrative scope the name promises, including token issuance, or (b) rename the scope to what it really is and make the refusal say which scope WOULD have been sufficient. Deleting history is routed, supported and default: POST /api/history/prune (app_history.py:17-21, days default 90) calls db_prune (db.py:1877-1900), which writes history and the history_fts index (db_fts_forget at db.py:1898) and never touches library; POST /api/batch/delete (app_batch.py:33) reaches batch_ops.bulk_delete (batch_ops.py:156) whose only history write is DELETE FROM history at batch_ops.py:185. library does declare FOREIGN KEY(history_id) REFERENCES history(id) ON DELETE SET NULL at migrations.py:403, but BD never sets PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON (stated at library.py:551-553), so the constraint is inert and the library row survives carrying a DANGLING history_id. With an absolute download dir the loss self-heals: the re-fetch at name_1 is recorded with an absolute file_path, db.py:1456 admits it, library_record backfills history.library_id (library.py:232 and 255), and the next run answers same for name_1 -- so the cost is exactly one extra full-size copy per url per prune event, not one per run. ARM B, non-absolute download dir: 4 runs of ONE url on the parent give exactly 4 transfers, exactly 4 mp4 files, exactly 0 library rows, and library_id NULL on all 4 history rows. GREEN requires, in both arms, that every run after the first performs exactly 0 additional transfers and leaves exactly 1 mp4 file; or, where attribution genuinely cannot be measured, that the run performs exactly 0 transfers and records a distinct non-done status instead of done, since UNKNOWN is never permission (CLAUDE.md A2 and A7). An arm whose transfer count cannot be measured reads UNKNOWN and the cut does not close. A scoped .github/dependabot.yml now ignores react-router MAJOR updates only -- every other update, including patch and minor for react-router itself, still flows -- so the same PR is not regenerated weekly and merged unexamined. THAT IGNORE ENTRY IS PART OF THIS ROW AND MUST BE DELETED WITH IT; leaving it in place after the migration would silently suppress the next genuine major. ACCEPTANCE: migrate to react-router 7 in a dedicated cut -- consolidate react-router-dom imports onto react-router, enumerate every routing API whose signature moved and state each one's disposition BEFORE changing it, and prove the result with the full frontend vitest suite, tsc --noEmit, a production build, and a route-by-route check that every path in the SPA still resolves; then confirm both advisories report resolved and remove the dependabot ignore entry in the same cut. DEPENDENCY: none, but it should not be batched with anything -- it touches every route in the product. |

NEGATIVE CONTROL: a well-formed nonempty script still replaces atomically exactly as today. EVIDENCE: bd-persist/FINDINGS_TRIAGE_2026-09-01.md F05. DEPENDENCY: none. |

| 626 | OPEN | BD-KILL-MINE-DOES-NOT-CHECK-PID-RECYCLING-BEFORE-SIGKILL -- F06, BUCKET UNKNOWN, MEASURED 2026-09-01 by the findings-triage workflow; re-verified live against the current /home/mboyle/bd-kill-mine.sh, which already carries the row-609 re-probe/classify fix (see row 619 for that overlap). WHAT IS UNMEASURED: whether this window is reachable in practice depends on this host's PID allocation behavior under load; /proc/sys/kernel/pid_max measured 4194304 on test5, and no fork-storm or deterministic PID-reuse reproduction was run, so whether an innocent process can actually receive the escalation SIGKILL during a realistic invocation remains unmeasured. ACCEPTANCE (what would settle it): a bounded reproduction -- either on an isolated low-pid_max host, or via a deterministic stat/kill seam (a test double for stat_of and kill forcing a PID-reuse condition between the initial match and the escalation kill) -- showing the escalation path signal an unrelated process reusing a matched PID; if reproduced, GREEN requires the signal-sending sites to re-check start time immediately before signaling and abstain (reporting the recycle) rather than firing. Until then this stays UNKNOWN, not closed, per CLAUDE.md A2. EVIDENCE: bd-persist/FINDINGS_TRIAGE_2026-09-01.md F06. DEPENDENCY: none; SUBJECT overlaps row 619 (F02, same file, distinct mechanism) and row 609 (already-implemented death re-probe). |
